A state is named by its path (`viewing.form.open`) and descriptions are left out, as they change nothing that runs. A flow one side lacks has no lines, so a flow added or removed whole shows every line; a screen added or removed whole is a change of the screens alone. The lines:

- `initial <state>`, and `<path> initial <state>` for each region;
- `guard <name> takes <code>, <code>`, one per guard by failure code, its codes as written, and `guard <name>: confirms <differs|strays|follows>` or `guard <name>: composite breaks`, one per guard a password pair derives; a guard's sentence is prose and has no line;
- `<path> address <path> <search as JSON, keys sorted>`, those the state's address has;
- `<path> renders <anchor>`, one per anchor of its `meta.renders` (`check-mail renders user.sign-in.resend-button`);
- `<path> entry|exit <effect>`;
- `<path> on <event> -> <target> [<guard>] <effects>`;
- `<path> invoke <action> <input as JSON, keys sorted> [read: false]`;
- `<path> onDone|onError [#<n>] -> <target> [<guard>] [read: false] <effects>`, a branch's `read: false` after its guard (`viewing.member-roles.changing onDone #1 -> idle [ownDemotion] read: false`);
- `<path> final`.

An effect is written `show <anchor>: <codes>`, `clear <anchor>`, `read <anchor>` or `navigate <to> <search as JSON>`. Where order runs the machine (the guarded alternatives of an event or an outcome, the effects of an `entry` or an `exit`), an item of a sequence of more than one carries its position, `#<n>`, and the lines are compared as sets. So a screen file written shorter that loads to the same flow shows no flow line.

A platform action is listed by its name, and its lines as `platform action lines added in <action>` and `platform action lines removed in <action>`, one per fact it declares (`actionLines(action)`, an internal helper): each parameter with its type or the value it refers to, the constraints it writes, each as JSON, and `optional` when it may be left out (`param email: string`, `param login: value email, maxLength 100`, `param kind: string, enum ["a","b"]`), its result (`result: <the text>`, the one prose that stays, since it says what holds once the action succeeds), each code its failure shows (`code rate-limited`), each role that may run it (`role <role>`), its page sizes when it pages (`paged by 10, 25, 50, 25 by default`), each read it changes (`changes organisations.invitations`), its mail (`mail to email language locale link user.verify-email?token`: who it goes to, the language it speaks, the screen its link opens and the search parameter that carries the token) and each notice (`notice password-changed to account language account link user.forgot-password`). An action added or removed whole shows every line, as nothing else in the summary says its mail.

### Written descriptions: their place and their length in the diff

<a id="rule-description-places"></a>
`readDescriptions(root)` reads every description the domain files write, as they write them, never one the engine derives or the loader fills in as it expands a shorter form: the screen files (a screen's own, its route parameters', its anchors', its container `states`, its flow's own, sentence guards, states, regions and transitions with the outcomes of invokes and of a region's button), the platform file (its values, its actions and their parameters, its `events`, its composites' own and their items), the activities (their own, their data, sentence guards, visits and transitions) and the model (its own, each entity's, action's and field's, the subschemas of the document, an entity or a field included), each read as YAML, the model's JSON too, so each has its line. Each is a `WrittenDescription`: its `file`, `line`, JSON path `at`, `kind` (the field it is, a `DescriptionKind`), `place` and `text`. The `Contract` carries them as `descriptions`, read by `loadContract`, so the contract at a git ref has those its files write there.

The place names a description alike at any ref, by what holds it, never by the depth of its path: `app-shell.theme-select`, `user.profile` (the screen's own), `user.profile state loading`, `user.profile flow`, `user.profile flow editing.save`, `user.delete-account flow deleting onError #2` (the second of its guarded alternatives), `user.profile guard nameChanged`, `user.sign-in binding /sign-in search email`, `platform action sign-up param email`, `platform event started`, `platform composite password-rules`, `model`, `model entity profile`, `model action profile.updateName`, `model field profile.name`, `activity session state profile on user.profile.sign-out-button`.

`descriptionChanges(before, after, renamed)` lists, sorted by place, each description that one side adds, removes or changes, a `DescriptionChange { place, before?, after? }` with its length in characters on each side it has, a renamed anchor's under its new name: `app-shell.theme-select 191 -> 340` (a shrink alike, `340 -> 191`), `new -> 120` for one added, `340 -> gone` for one removed, and the same length on both sides for a text changed at its length. It prints last, as `descriptions changed: <place> <before> -> <after>, ...`, so a description that grows clause by clause shows on review even where nothing that runs changed. Each description keeps the budget of its field (see the rule `check-files`).

### Handing over a change

<a id="rule-hand-over"></a>
A change to the contract goes with the output of `bun run contract:diff main` in its description (the screens, anchors, codes, entities, fields and activities added, removed or renamed, the composites added or removed, the derived parts, the codes, the states, the roles and the error fields the anchors both sides hold gain or lose, the messages among them that rank their codes, with their order, who each route serves, the search parameters of each route, the platform action each list reads with its params and its paging, the platform actions added or removed and the notices they declare, the transitions added or removed in each activity both sides hold, the lines of the flow added or removed in each screen both sides hold with a flow, the lines of each platform action added, removed or changed, the reads each action of the model comes to change or stops changing, and each written description added, removed or changed with its length before and after), and below it, by hand, the reason for each rename or removal, since those break whoever reads the old name:

```text
<the output of bun run contract:diff main>

Why:
- <each anchor, code, screen, entity, field, activity or transition renamed or removed>: <the reason>
Scenarios and tests changed with it: <scenario ids>
```

For example, renaming the revoke button of the devices page:

```text
contract changes: 1 anchor renamed
anchors renamed: device.list.revoke-button -> device.list.sign-out-button

Why:
- device.list.revoke-button: the button signs the device out, and the anchor now says so
Scenarios and tests changed with it: device/base/my-devices
```

The tests are in this repository, so the same change updates them, and the consumers check fails until it does. This is the delivery of a finished change; the review before the code is the owner's process of work.

### Coverage: how far the contract reaches

<a id="rule-coverage"></a>
`bun run contract:coverage` (`ui-contract coverage [--write | --check] [--json] [--unmarked] [--descriptions]`) prints how far the contract reaches, and `--write` puts the same block into `COVERAGE.md`, between the markers `<!-- coverage:start -->` and `<!-- coverage:end -->` (`COVERAGE_START`, `COVERAGE_END`): the text outside them is written by hand, and `replaceCoverageBlock(note, block)` throws when the note lacks one marker of each, in that order. It is a report, not a gate: no measure fails a commit. The block stays current, though: `bun run contract:coverage:check` (`ui-contract coverage --check`, `coverageDrift(note, block)`) fails when the block in `COVERAGE.md` is not what `--write` would write, naming that command at the line of its start marker, and says so in one line when it is. It runs in `bun run check` and in the pre-commit hook on a commit on `main`; on a branch, the contract trunk included, the hook leaves it out, so a block left behind fails `bun run check` before a landing, and the squash on `main`.

1. **The model**, the measure of completeness (`modelCoverage`): per entity of `model.schema.json`, each field and each action with the anchors whose `binding` (one or a list) names it, or `unbound`. A field may be internal (an id, a creation time), so an unbound one is a question, not an error. Then per activity (`activityCoverage`), the scenarios that name it in their `activity` and the transitions they walk, one per branch of each `on`: a scenario is read in the order of its file (`where`, the steps and the expected results, each text's references left to right), with an address per device, a text that opens with `On device X` or `Device X:` speaking for device X, any other for the scenario's only device or for the device last named (`A` at first); a route moves its device to that address, and an anchor named in a step walks the transition on it from every state at its device's address, or from the initial state while that device has named no route. The totals line adds `activities n, transitions walked x/y`.
2. **The controls**, the secondary measure (`countControls(text, file)`): per feature module of the web app (`FEATURES_DIR`) and for the UI kit (`UI_KIT_DIR`, `UI_KIT_MODULE`), how many controls carry an anchor, which are left unmarked and which are declared off the coverage. A control is a JSX element named like one (`CONTROL_TAGS`) or any element with an `onClick`; it is marked when it carries a `data-testid`, `testId` or `*TestId` prop, or sits inside a `<label>`; an element inside a marked control without an action of its own is a part of that control and is not counted, and neither is one that passes on the props it receives. `--unmarked` lists the unmarked and the ignored ones as `file:line: <element>`. The count is an indicator read from the source text, not a rule of the contract.

The block also lists, per screen, its anchors and the scenarios that reach it. Its totals line reads `fields bound x/y, actions bound x/y`, then `activities n, transitions walked x/y` when the contract has any, with a table of them after the model, before the controls marked and ignored. After the totals, a note names each composite the platform declares and no screen opts into (`Note: composite <name> is declared and used by no screen.`, `unusedComposites`), which fails nothing.

A control that is not a test target is declared off the coverage by a marker right before its element, with only white space between, and its reason on the marker line itself, at least three characters long (`// ui-contract-ignore-self: no screen renders a sheet yet; the first one anchors it`). In code it is a line comment, `// ui-contract-ignore: <reason>`; among JSX children it is the only content of a comment container, `{/* ui-contract-ignore: <reason> */}`. `ui-contract-ignore` covers the element and everything inside it, `ui-contract-ignore-self` the element alone. A marker without a reason, in another form, not right before an element, or that ignores no control (its element, and for `ui-contract-ignore` everything inside it, holds none) makes the command fail and write nothing (see the rule `check-sources`).

`bun run contract:coverage --descriptions` (`listDescriptions(contract)`) lists as `<anchor>: <description>` every written description equal to the one the engine says itself, for the developer to drop; an anchor a composite derives is never listed (see the rule `engine-descriptions`).

### Language

<a id="rule-language"></a>
The page renders `en` on the stands, and its wording is not part of the contract. Assert `data-code` (`contract.codes`), `data-state`, `data-id` and `data-value`, never text; the only text a test reads is text it entered itself.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.language`](#manifest-contract-instructions-language).

### Sessions and platform actions

<a id="rule-session"></a>
Every scenario starts in a fresh browser context with nobody signed in (`guest`). What a platform action does is read from `platform.actions.<name>`, the platform file: its `description`, its `result` (what holds once it succeeds), its `codes` (what a failure shows), its `changes` (written on every action, `[]` where it changes none: the reads whose answer, for the organisation, the account and the params it ran with, differs once it succeeds, platform actions a list, a section or a table takes as its `source`) and, when it sends mails, its `mail` and its `notices`; never assume it from the action's name. One account per browser: a second account, or a second device of the same one, needs a browser context of its own.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.session`](#manifest-contract-instructions-session).

### Reading the screens

<a id="rule-screen-map"></a>
`screens` lists every screen once. Its `id` is the screen name its anchors start with; its `title` is what a reader calls it and its `description` what it is for. `bindings` are its routes in the router's format (TanStack): `path` with static segments, `$param`, `{-$param}` (optional) and `$` (splat); `guard` (`guest` or `authenticated`: where an account the route is not for is sent, a guest to sign-in or a signed-in account to the profile); `roles` (the roles it serves: the router serves it only to an account that holds one, after its `guard` and before it reads the search, and opens the screen of the binding at `/$`, the not-found screen, at the same address for any other); `params` and `search` as flat JSON Schemas; `history` (`push` or `replace`); `retain` (search parameters kept across navigations); `example`, a deep link to the route with fake data, written as the router writes a link: each path parameter at its first `examples` value, every search key at its `default`, else its first `enum` value or example (the app's own links leave out a value equal to its default and reach the same state). `frame: true` marks the frame around every screen, which has no route. Every binding `path` is a TanStack Router path in the router's own syntax, `params` are its path parameters and `search` its search parameters, and a route is a state address: the same path with the same search reaches the same state after a reload. `entry` lists the anchors present once the screen is open; some are alternatives by state, so wait for the one the state shows. `anchors` maps each declared anchor to its `kind`, `binding`, `codes` (its `data-code` values), `fields` (an error's codes, each with the field it concerns, which the error shows under and marks invalid), `dataId` (what its `data-id` holds), `dataValue` (it or its options carry a `data-value` of `contract.choiceValues`), `states` (its `data-state` values), `derived` (the suffixes it renders as `<anchor>.<suffix>`), `roles` (who sees it, among the roles its screen's bindings serve; absent: every role of the screen), `own` (shown only on the entries the member owns), `with` and `without` (it shows only while each `with` entry shows and no `without` entry does; an entry is an anchor of its screen, or `{ "<anchor>": [<code>, …] }`, that anchor while it shows one of these codes; an anchor rendered in each entry of a list names anchors of the same entry), `ranked` (a message whose `codes` are in rank order: of the codes raised before it shows, it shows the first, and the others go with it; a `clear` of it, in any flow, takes away every code raised before it), `secret` (a field that holds a secret, a password: it never leaves the page but in the request of the action that takes it, never into an address and never filled from one) and `source` (where a list's entries come from: a platform action, which the list reads each time it starts to render after it did not, each time the organisation or account it reads for becomes another or a search parameter it reads does, and where a flow says `read`, with `params` (each a search parameter of the route, `{ "search": "<name>" }`, or a constant) and `paged: true` (one page of the action's entries: the page and the size the route's search parameters `page` and `size` name; the list derives `range`, whose `data-from`, `data-to` and `data-total` say which entries the page shows of how many, `previous-link` and `next-link`, each only where there is such a page, and `size-select`, whose options carry the action's `paged.sizes` and choose the route's `size`, which writes no `x-perch`; the select holds no input, so its element carries `holdsInput={false}`; a paged list has at most 3 segments, as its options add two to its name); or an entity of the model, which it follows as the stored data changes). A screen's `states` say what each container state shows, and its `wireframe` is the default state as a Playwright aria snapshot. A screen's `flow` is an XState v5 machine config: `initial`, `states` with `on` (an event is the user acting on that anchor, typing included for a field, a platform event of `platform.events`, or a data event `model.<entity>.<field>`: the stored value of a field the screen shows changed, here or on another device), `target`, `guard` (a name of `meta.guards`), `actions` (effects `show`, `clear`, `read` and `navigate`: `clear` empties a field of the screen, a secret one included, so it holds no input any more, as if nothing was typed, and makes an anchor that carries codes show none until one is shown again; `read` makes a list of the screen whose `source` is a platform action read that action again and show its entries once the read is in, the app taking them from the answer of the action that just ran when it returns them; such a list also reads it each time it starts to render after it did not and each time the organisation or account it reads for becomes another or a search parameter it reads does, and a navigate that sets a search parameter a paged list reads says `page: 1`; one whose `source` is an entity follows the stored data and is never read; a navigate's `search` gives each key a literal, `{ "anchor": "<field>" }`, the value that field of the screen holds when the app moves, or `{ "item": "<item>" }`, the `data-id` of the entry of that item or row whose control the user acted on, a control (a button, a link, a tab or a field) in the item's part (`<part>.<name>`) on whose event the navigate runs; an item or a row as a value of `params` or `input` gives the `data-id` of the entry whose control acts, likewise), `invoke` (a model or platform action with its `input`, whose values are as a binding's `params`, `onDone`, `onError`; `read: false` on an invoke says nothing reads after its action, which changes nothing a list of the screen shows, and `read: false` on one branch of its `onDone` or `onError` says nothing reads after that outcome, where another branch reads; never both, never beside a `read` of the same branch, never on the transition of an event; after an invoke that does not say `read: false`, every branch of its `onDone` reads each list of the screen with a platform source that its action may change, or says `read: false`: a list one of whose entries its `input` takes, one whose source's `params` take a value its `input` takes, and one whose source its action's `changes` names; a failure holds no read, so an `onError` reads where it says), `type: final`, and `meta.address` on a state a route reaches. A state of the root may be `type: parallel`: its `states` are regions, the screen's independent areas, all active at once, each with its `initial` and its plain states; a target of the root may name a region's state as `<parallel>.<region>.<state>` (the other regions start at their `initial`), a target in a region names a state of that region or, by the flow's `id` (a one-segment name the flow declares, which the app gives its machine), a state of the root as `#<flow id>.<state>`, which leaves the parallel state and enters that state afresh, and within a parallel state an event is handled by one region or by the state's own `on`, never by two. A state's `meta.renders` names anchors of the screen that only some states render: an anchor no state of the flow lists is rendered in every state, one that a state lists is rendered only in the states that list it, a state of a region rendering what its parallel state lists too; the anchors nested under a listed one (`<anchor>.<rest>`, such as the items of a list) are rendered with it, or where they are listed themselves. Rendered means visible to the user: an anchor a state does not render is absent from the page or hidden on it. A guard of `meta.guards` is a sentence saying when it holds, or `{ "codes": [<code>, …] }`, which guards only a branch of an invoke's `onError` and holds when its action failed with one of those codes. Carry it into `setup({ guards, actions, actors }).createMachine(flow)` unchanged, implementing the guards it names, the four effects and an actor per `invoke.src`; an invoke's `read: false`, or a branch's, is a statement of the contract, nothing the machine runs. A control whose anchor is an event of the flow (a button, a select, a switch) is enabled exactly while a state the machine is in handles that event (`snapshot.can`), and disabled otherwise: a Save disabled while it saves is the flow saying so, never a flag of its own. A `visibility-button` (`<input>.visibility-button`, the eye of a secret input, in `contract.derivedParts`) shows what the input holds as typed, or hides it again: presentation only, it is never an event of a flow and always enabled, and its `data-value` names what a click does, `show` or `hide`. `ids` holds the same anchors flat, each with its screen. On a development or e2e build, `window.__perch.ui.anchors()` lists the anchors the page renders now, with their attributes and visibility. `composite` on a secret input names the platform composite it opts into (`platform.composites.<name>`): the screen's `anchors` then hold its list and items, each marked `derivedFrom` with the composite and, for an item, its name; they are declared anchors like any other.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.screenMap`](#manifest-contract-instructions-screenmap).

### Field states

<a id="rule-field-states"></a>
A field carries `data-state` `unsaved` while it holds input no save has taken; one whose `fieldStates` lists `saved` carries `saved` once a save stored what it shows, while it stays on the screen; a field carries no `data-state` otherwise.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.screenMap`](#manifest-contract-instructions-screenmap).

### Bindings

<a id="rule-bindings"></a>
`{ "model": "<entity>.<field>" }` shows or edits `model.$defs.<entity>.properties.<field>`. `{ "action": "<entity>.<action>" }` runs an action of `model.$defs.<entity>.x-perch.actions`; `{ "action": "platform.<name>" }` runs `platform.actions.<name>`. Its `params` name, per parameter, the anchor whose value it takes, a constant `{ "value": ... }`, or `{ "search": "<name>" }`, the value of that search parameter of the route the screen was opened at. An action of `platform.actions` that sends a mail declares it under `mail`: `to` (the parameter whose value it goes to, or `account`: the email of the account the action acts on, the signed-in account for an action on its own account, else the one its parameters name, whoever is signed in), `language` (a parameter holding a language, `account`, or `recipient`: the account whose email the mail goes to, else the signed-in account) and `link` (the `screen` its link opens and the `search` parameter carrying its token); a scenario names it `[mail:<action>]`. Its `notices` are mails that only tell, with no token, each with its `name`, `to` (a parameter, or `account`: the email of the account the action acts on, as it was before the action changed anything), `language` and, optionally, a plain `link` to a `screen` whose routes need no parameter; a scenario names one `[notice:<action>.<name>]`. `{ "route": "/path" }` navigates to the path of a binding of some screen. `{ "search": "<name>" }` on a field starts it with the value of that search parameter of the route the screen was opened at and gives it that value again each time the parameter becomes another, which the user may change; it may sit in a list beside the action the field gives its value to. A list of bindings runs in its order, or one of them by the mode of the screen; the `flow` says which. An anchor with `asks` asks the frame's confirmation (`asks.dialog`, with `asks.code`) before its action runs: the app's platform gate opens it, confirm runs the action and cancel does not; a screen builds no dialog of its own for it. An anchor with `leaves` is a control whose flow moves to another screen: the app's platform gate asks the frame's leave question (`leaves.dialog`) before its event while one of the fields of `leaves.loses` holds unsaved input, leave goes on and stay does not; the fields the move carries in its search are not among them. A button's action `params` are the union of the params the fields of its part pass for that action, a param the screen writes on the button winning; an error's `codes`, unless the screen writes them, are the failures of the actions bound beside it and invoked against it by the flow, plus the codes the flow shows on it, `failed` last; an invoke without `onError` whose action fails with codes clears its part's error on entry and, back in the one state that entered it on a control, shows the failure there (a start state, one several states enter, or one a platform or data event enters, writes its `onError`), and an `onError` written as its target alone still shows the failure on that error, on the screen's error when no control enters and the input names no field.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.bindings`](#manifest-contract-instructions-bindings).

### Reading the model

<a id="rule-model"></a>
`model` is the data model: a JSON Schema 2020-12 document with one `$defs` entry per entity. An entity's `x-perch` says its `table`, its `region` (`private`: the account's own data; `tenant`: the current organisation's), its `id` prefix, its `owner` field, `can` (per role, the actions allowed on any entry or only on its own), its `actions` with their parameters and their `changes` (written on every action, as a platform action writes it), and `unique` and `index`. A field's `x-perch` says whether it is `readonly`, the entity a `record` points at, and its `crdt`: how concurrent edits merge (`text`, `list`, `counter`, or `none` for the last writer).

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.model`](#manifest-contract-instructions-model).

### Reading the activities

<a id="rule-activities"></a>
`activities` are the activities across screens, one file each in `contract/domain/activities/<id>.yaml`, carried here verbatim: which screens form one task, in which order, and what the task carries from one screen to the next. An activity has an `id`, a `title`, a `description` (what it is for), the `roles` that take part and `data` (what it carries, by name, each with a `description` and, when it is a field of the model, its `model` as `<entity>.<field>`). The rest is an XState v5 machine config in the subset of the flows, without effects or `invoke`: `initial`, `meta.guards` and `states`, where a state is a visit to a screen: `meta.screen` names the screen (one screen may be visited by several states), `meta.address` the `path` of a binding of that screen (the activity is where its address is), and `type: final` ends the activity. A state's `on` maps an anchor of its screen or of the frame to where acting on it leads: `target` (the next visit), `guard` and `description`. The activity says where a task leads and the screens do the work: after that anchor, the flow of its screen navigates to the target's address, or the anchor is a link to it (`binding.route`). Carry each into `setup({ guards }).createMachine(activity)` unchanged, as the flows, implementing the guards it names; a scenario that walks an activity names it in its `activity`.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.activities`](#manifest-contract-instructions-activities).

### Reading the scenarios

<a id="rule-scenarios"></a>
`scenarios` are the end-to-end scenarios in the spec-instruction shape, authored as Markdown files in the e2e tree (`tests/e2e/scenarios/<entity>/<layer>/[<area>/]<slug>.md`: a YAML front matter with `tags`, an optional `activity`, `devices` and `data`, then the sections `# <title>`, `Where:`, `## Scenario:`, `### Steps` and `### Expected result`) and carried here parsed. Their fields: `id` (the file's path under `tests/e2e/scenarios/` without `.md`, `<entity>/<layer>/[<area>/]<slug>` with the layer one of `base`, `flows`, `features`; the test is `tests/e2e/src/<id>.e2e.spec.ts`), `title`, `tags` (`smoke` or `full`), `activity` (the id of the activity of `activities` it walks, when it walks one), `devices` (a letter per browser with the role it reaches by the end; every device starts as `guest`), `where`, `data` (`generate` or a fixed value), `scenario`, `steps` and `expected`. Brackets in their text reference the contract: `[<anchor>]` (in `ids`, or derived from one with a suffix of its `derived`), `[/path]` (a route of `screens`), `[code:<code>]`, `[state:<state>]`, `[value:<value>]`, `[mail:<action>]` (a platform action that declares the mail it sends) and `[notice:<action>.<name>]` (a notice of that name the platform action declares). They are the contract's feedback loop: a scenario that cannot be written from the contract alone (an anchor, a code, a state, a route or a role it would need) names a gap to fix in the contract files, never one to work around in a test.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.scenarios`](#manifest-contract-instructions-scenarios).

### Contract gaps

<a id="rule-contract-gaps"></a>
When the contract cannot say what you need (a state, a region, an event, a value, a route, a role, a code, a shape), stop at that part: build what the contract covers and report a contract gap with your result, as four lines: `Contract gap:` what is needed; `Facet:` which part of the contract cannot say it (the model, a screen file, the platform, the flow subset, an activity, the scenario grammar, a check); `Emulation refused:` the workaround you did not build; `Proposal:` the change to the contract and its cost. Never hold it in the app or in a test instead: state in a component for a flow state or a region the contract lacks, a value or a route it does not carry, an anchor or a code born in the code or in a test. An emulation is a fork of the model that no check sees and the next build from this manifest loses; the contract is changed first. The one transient state a component holds is whether a password input shows what it holds, which the `visibility-button` the contract declares on it toggles.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.gaps`](#manifest-contract-instructions-gaps).
